<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Politics">Politics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Politics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Guinea" title="Politics of Guinea">Politics of Guinea</a></div>
<p>Guinea is a republic. The president is directly elected by the people and is the <a href="/wiki/Head_of_state" title="Head of state">head of state</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Head_of_government" title="Head of government">head of government</a>. The unicameral National Assembly is the <a href="/wiki/Legislative_body" class="mw-redirect" title="Legislative body">legislative body</a> of the country, and its members are directly elected by the people. The judicial branch is headed by the <a href="/w/index.php?title=Supreme_Court_of_Guinea&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Supreme Court of Guinea (page does not exist)">Supreme Court of Guinea</a>, the highest and final court of appeal in the country.<sup id="cite_ref-reuters.com_74-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-reuters.com-74">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/National_Assembly_of_Guinea" class="mw-redirect" title="National Assembly of Guinea">National Assembly of Guinea</a>, the country's legislative body, did not meet from 2008 to 2013, when it was dissolved after the military coup in December. <a href="/wiki/Guinean_legislative_election,_2013" class="mw-redirect" title="Guinean legislative election, 2013">Elections</a> have been postponed multiple times since 2007. In April 2012, President Condé postponed the elections indefinitely, citing the need to ensure that they were "transparent and democratic".<sup id="cite_ref-RNW_Africa_Desk_75-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RNW_Africa_Desk-75">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Guinean_legislative_election,_2013" class="mw-redirect" title="Guinean legislative election, 2013">2013 Guinean legislative election</a> was held on 24 September.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> President <a href="/wiki/Alpha_Cond%C3%A9" title="Alpha Condé">Alpha Condé</a>'s party, the <a href="/wiki/Rally_of_the_Guinean_People" title="Rally of the Guinean People">Rally of the Guinean People</a> (RPG), won a <a href="/wiki/Plurality_(voting)" title="Plurality (voting)">plurality</a> of seats in the <a href="/wiki/National_Assembly_of_Guinea" class="mw-redirect" title="National Assembly of Guinea">National Assembly of Guinea</a>, with 53 out of 114 seats. The opposition parties won a total of 53 seats, and opposition leaders denounced the official results as fraudulent.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/President_of_Guinea" class="mw-redirect" title="President of Guinea">president of Guinea</a> is normally elected by popular vote for a 5-year term; the winning candidate must receive a majority of the votes cast to be elected president. The president governs Guinea, assisted by a <a href="/wiki/Privy_Council" class="mw-redirect" title="Privy Council">council</a> of 25 civilian <a href="/wiki/Minister_(government)" title="Minister (government)">ministers</a>, appointed by him. The government administers the country through 8 regions, 33 <a href="/wiki/Prefecture" title="Prefecture">prefectures</a>, over 100 <a href="/wiki/Subprefecture" title="Subprefecture">subprefectures</a>, and districts (known as <a href="/wiki/Commune_(subnational_entity)" class="mw-redirect" title="Commune (subnational entity)">communes</a> in Conakry and other cities and villages, or <i>quartiers</i> in the interior). District-level leaders are elected; the president appoints officials to all other levels of the centralized administration. Former President <a href="/wiki/Alpha_Cond%C3%A9" title="Alpha Condé">Alpha Condé</a> derived support from Guinea's second-largest ethnic group, the <a href="/wiki/Malinke" class="mw-redirect" title="Malinke">Malinke</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Guinea_2013_77-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Guinea_2013-77">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> Guinea's opposition was backed by the <a href="/wiki/Fula_people" title="Fula people">Fula</a> ethnic group,<sup id="cite_ref-Fula_78-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Fula-78">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> who account for around 33.4% of the population.<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_relations">Foreign relations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Foreign relations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Guinea" title="Foreign relations of Guinea">Foreign relations of Guinea</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Vladimir_Putin_and_Alpha_Cond%C3%A9_(2017-09-28)_2.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f1/Vladimir_Putin_and_Alpha_Cond%C3%A9_%282017-09-28%29_2.jpg/220px-Vladimir_Putin_and_Alpha_Cond%C3%A9_%282017-09-28%29_2.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="136" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f1/Vladimir_Putin_and_Alpha_Cond%C3%A9_%282017-09-28%29_2.jpg/330px-Vladimir_Putin_and_Alpha_Cond%C3%A9_%282017-09-28%29_2.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f1/Vladimir_Putin_and_Alpha_Cond%C3%A9_%282017-09-28%29_2.jpg/440px-Vladimir_Putin_and_Alpha_Cond%C3%A9_%282017-09-28%29_2.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1880" data-file-height="1160" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Vladimir_Putin_and_Alpha_Cond%C3%A9_(2017-09-28)_2.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>President <a href="/wiki/Alpha_Cond%C3%A9" title="Alpha Condé">Alpha Condé</a> with Russian President <a href="/wiki/Vladimir_Putin" title="Vladimir Putin">Vladimir Putin</a> on 28 September 2017</div></div></div>
<p>Guinea is a member of the <a href="/wiki/African_Union" title="African Union">African Union</a>, <a href="/wiki/Agency_for_the_French-Speaking_Community" class="mw-redirect" title="Agency for the French-Speaking Community">Agency for the French-Speaking Community</a>, <a href="/wiki/African_Development_Bank" title="African Development Bank">African Development Bank</a>, <a href="/wiki/Economic_Community_of_West_African_States" title="Economic Community of West African States">Economic Community of West African States</a>, <a href="/wiki/International_Bank_for_Reconstruction_and_Development" title="International Bank for Reconstruction and Development">World Bank</a>, <a href="/wiki/Islamic_Development_Bank" title="Islamic Development Bank">Islamic Development Bank</a>, <a href="/wiki/International_Monetary_Fund" title="International Monetary Fund">IMF</a>, and the United Nations.
</p><p>According to a February 2009 <a href="/wiki/U.S._Department_of_State" class="mw-redirect" title="U.S. Department of State">U.S. Department of State</a> statement, Guinea's foreign relations, including those with its West African neighbours, had improved steadily since 1985.<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> The Department's October 2018 statement indicated that—although "the U.S. condemned" Guinea's "2008 military coup d'etat,"—the U.S. had "close relations" with Guinea before the coup, and after "Guinea's presidential elections in 2010, the United States re-established strong diplomatic relations with the government." The statement indicated support for the "legislative elections in 2013 and a second presidential election in 2015," as signs of "democratic reform."<sup id="cite_ref-us_relations_2018_10_30_state_gov_81-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-us_relations_2018_10_30_state_gov-81">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> A March 2021 report by the U.S. State Department blasted extensive human rights violations by the government, security forces and businesses in Guinea. The report cited extensive international criticism of the recent national elections, which yielded "President Alpha Conde's re-election (despite disputed results)... following a controversial March referendum amending the constitution and allowing him to run for a third term."<sup id="cite_ref-human_rights_guinea_2021_03_30_state_gov_82-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-human_rights_guinea_2021_03_30_state_gov-82">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> The Department condemned the 2021 coup, warning that "violence and any extra-constitutional measures will only erode Guinea's prospects for peace, stability, and prosperity, [and] could limit the ability of the United States and Guinea's other international partners to support the country...," While not explicitly calling for President Condé's return to power, the U.S. called for "national dialogue to address concerns sustainably and transparently to enable a peaceful and democratic way forward for Guinea..."<sup id="cite_ref-us_relations_2021_09_05_state_gov_83-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-us_relations_2021_09_05_state_gov-83">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-guinea_coup_leader_2021_09_06_reuters_84-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-guinea_coup_leader_2021_09_06_reuters-84">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The United Nations promptly denounced the 2021 coup, and some of Guinea's allies condemned the coup. The <a href="/wiki/African_Union" title="African Union">African Union</a> and West Africa's regional bloc (<a href="/wiki/ECOWAS" class="mw-redirect" title="ECOWAS">ECOWAS</a>), both threatened sanctions—while some analysts expect the threats to be of limited effect because Guinea is not a member of the West African currency union, and is not a landlocked country.<sup id="cite_ref-guinea_coup_leader_2021_09_06_reuters_84-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-guinea_coup_leader_2021_09_06_reuters-84">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> ECOWAS promptly suspended Guinea's membership, and demanded the unconditional release of President Condé, while sending envoys to Conakry to attempt a "constitutional" resolution of the situation.<sup id="cite_ref-leaders_due_in_guinea_2021_09_09_reuters_65-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-leaders_due_in_guinea_2021_09_09_reuters-65">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-leaders_suspend_guinea_2021_09_08_bbc_66-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-leaders_suspend_guinea_2021_09_08_bbc-66">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> Uncharacteristically responding to another nation's internal affairs, China (which relies on Guinea for half of its aluminium ore, facilitated by connections to ousted President Condé) openly opposed the coup.<sup id="cite_ref-china_is_ok_2021_09_foreign_policy_67-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-china_is_ok_2021_09_foreign_policy-67">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Military">Military</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Military">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Military_of_Guinea" class="mw-redirect" title="Military of Guinea">Military of Guinea</a></div>
<p>Guinea's armed forces are divided into 5 branches—army, navy, air force, the paramilitary National <a href="/wiki/Gendarmerie" title="Gendarmerie">Gendarmerie</a> and the Republican Guard—whose chiefs report to the Chairman of the Joint Chiefs of Staff who is subordinate to the Minister of Defence. In addition, regime security forces include the National Police Force (Sûreté Nationale). The Gendarmerie, responsible for internal security, has a strength of several thousand.
</p><p>The army, with about 15,000 personnel, is by far the largest branch of the armed forces and is mainly responsible for protecting the state borders, the security of administered territories, and defending Guinea's national interests. Air force personnel total about 700. Its equipment includes several Russian-supplied fighter planes and transports. The navy has about 900 personnel and operates several small <a href="/wiki/Patrol_craft" class="mw-redirect" title="Patrol craft">patrol craft</a> and barges.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Human_rights">Human rights</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Human rights">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Human_rights_in_Guinea" title="Human rights in Guinea">Human rights in Guinea</a></div>
<p>Homosexuality is illegal in Guinea.<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> The prime minister declared in 2010 that he does not consider sexual orientation a legitimate human right.<sup id="cite_ref-State_2011_21-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-State_2011-21">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Guinea has one of the world's highest rates of <a href="/wiki/Female_genital_mutilation" title="Female genital mutilation">female genital mutilation</a> (FGM, sometimes referred to as 'female circumcision') according to Anastasia Gage, an associate professor at <a href="/wiki/Tulane_University" title="Tulane University">Tulane University</a>, and Ronan van Rossem, an associate professor at <a href="/wiki/Ghent_University" title="Ghent University">Ghent University</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> Female genital mutilation in Guinea had been performed on more than 98% of women as of 2009<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Guinea&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>.<sup id="cite_ref-The_effects_of_female_genital_mutil_87-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_effects_of_female_genital_mutil-87">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> In Guinea "almost all cultures, religions, and ethnicities" practice female genital mutilation.<sup id="cite_ref-The_effects_of_female_genital_mutil_87-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_effects_of_female_genital_mutil-87">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> The 2005 <a href="/wiki/Demographic_and_Health_Survey" class="mw-redirect" title="Demographic and Health Survey">Demographic and Health Survey</a> reported that 96% of women have gone through the operation.<sup id="cite_ref-State_2011_21-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-State_2011-21">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Guinea" title="Economy of Guinea">Economy of Guinea</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:252px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Fisher_women_on_River_Niger_in_Guinea,_Africa.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/14/Fisher_women_on_River_Niger_in_Guinea%2C_Africa.jpg/250px-Fisher_women_on_River_Niger_in_Guinea%2C_Africa.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/14/Fisher_women_on_River_Niger_in_Guinea%2C_Africa.jpg/375px-Fisher_women_on_River_Niger_in_Guinea%2C_Africa.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/14/Fisher_women_on_River_Niger_in_Guinea%2C_Africa.jpg/500px-Fisher_women_on_River_Niger_in_Guinea%2C_Africa.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4272" data-file-height="2828" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Fisher_women_on_River_Niger_in_Guinea,_Africa.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Malinke fisher women on the Niger River, <a href="/wiki/Niandankoro" title="Niandankoro">Niandankoro</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kankan_Region" title="Kankan Region">Kankan Region</a>, in eastern Guinea</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:252px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Kissidougou_market.JPG" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/Kissidougou_market.JPG/250px-Kissidougou_market.JPG" decoding="async" width="250" height="167" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/Kissidougou_market.JPG/375px-Kissidougou_market.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/Kissidougou_market.JPG/500px-Kissidougou_market.JPG 2x" data-file-width="3072" data-file-height="2048" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Kissidougou_market.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Kissidougou" title="Kissidougou">Kissidougou</a> market</div></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Agriculture">Agriculture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Agriculture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The agriculture sector at some point employed approximately 75% of the country. The rice is cultivated in the flooded zones between streams and rivers. The local production of rice is not sufficient to feed the country, so rice is imported from Asia. The sector cultivates coffee beans, pineapples, peaches, nectarines, <a href="/wiki/Mango" title="Mango">mangoes</a>, oranges, bananas, potatoes, tomatoes, cucumbers, pepper, and other types of produce. Guinea is one of the emerging regional producers of apples and pears. There are plantations of grapes, pomegranates, and more recent years have seen the development of strawberry plantations, based on the vertical hydroponic system.<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Natural_resources">Natural resources</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Natural resources">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Guinea has 25% or more of the world's known <a href="/wiki/Bauxite" title="Bauxite">bauxite</a> reserves. It has diamonds, gold, and other metals. Bauxite and <a href="/wiki/Aluminium_oxide" title="Aluminium oxide">alumina</a> are the most major exports. Other industries include processing plants for beer, juices, soft drinks and tobacco. Agriculture employs 75% of the nation's labour force. Under French rule, and at the beginning of independence, Guinea was an exporter of <a href="/wiki/Bananas" class="mw-redirect" title="Bananas">bananas</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pineapples" class="mw-redirect" title="Pineapples">pineapples</a>, coffee, peanuts, and <a href="/wiki/Palm_oil" title="Palm oil">palm oil</a>. Soil, water, and climatic conditions provide opportunities for irrigated farming and agro industry.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Mining">Mining</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Mining">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Mining_industry_of_Guinea" title="Mining industry of Guinea">Mining industry of Guinea</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:292px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Guinea_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/31/Guinea_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/290px-Guinea_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="290" height="189" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/31/Guinea_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/435px-Guinea_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/31/Guinea_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/580px-Guinea_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Guinea_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A proportional representation of Guinea exports, 2019</div></div></div>
<p>Guinea possesses over 25&#160;billion <a href="/wiki/Tonne" title="Tonne">tonnes</a> (metric tons) of <a href="/wiki/Bauxite" title="Bauxite">bauxite</a> – and perhaps up to one half of the world's reserves. Its mineral wealth includes more than 4-billion tonnes of high-grade iron ore, and diamond and gold deposits, and <a href="/wiki/Uranium" title="Uranium">uranium</a>. Possibilities for investment and commercial activities exist in all these areas, and Guinea's "poorly developed infrastructure and rampant corruption continue to present obstacles to large-scale investment projects".<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>
Joint venture bauxite mining and alumina operations in north-west Guinea historically provide about 80% of Guinea's <a href="/wiki/Foreign_exchange_reserves" title="Foreign exchange reserves">Foreign exchange reserves</a>. Bauxite is refined into <a href="/wiki/Alumina" class="mw-redirect" title="Alumina">alumina</a>, which is later <a href="/wiki/Smelting" title="Smelting">smelted</a> into aluminium. The <i><a href="/wiki/Compagnie_des_Bauxites_de_Guin%C3%A9e" title="Compagnie des Bauxites de Guinée">Compagnie des Bauxites de Guinée</a></i> (CBG) exports about 14 million tonnes of high-grade bauxite annually. CBG is a joint venture, 49% owned by the Guinean government and 51% by an international consortium known as <a href="/wiki/Halco_Mining" title="Halco Mining">Halco Mining</a> Inc., itself a joint venture controlled by aluminium producer <a href="/wiki/Alcoa" title="Alcoa">Alcoa</a> (AA), global miner <a href="/wiki/Rio_Tinto_Group" class="mw-redirect" title="Rio Tinto Group">Rio Tinto Group</a> and Dadco Investments.<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> CBG has exclusive rights to bauxite reserves and resources in north-western Guinea, through 2038.<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> In 2008, protesters upset about poor electrical services blocked the tracks CBG uses. Guinea includes a proviso in its agreements with international oil companies, requiring its partners to generate power for nearby communities.<sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup></p><div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:252px;"><a href="/wiki/File:River_sand_production_Guinea.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/64/River_sand_production_Guinea.jpg/250px-River_sand_production_Guinea.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="188" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/64/River_sand_production_Guinea.jpg/375px-River_sand_production_Guinea.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/64/River_sand_production_Guinea.jpg/500px-River_sand_production_Guinea.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1280" data-file-height="960" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:River_sand_production_Guinea.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Children engaged in manual labour in order to support their families.</div></div></div><p>The <i>Compagnie des Bauxites de Kindia</i> (CBK), a joint venture between the government of Guinea and <a href="/wiki/United_Company_RUSAL" class="mw-redirect" title="United Company RUSAL">RUSAL</a>, produces some 2.5&#160;million <a href="/wiki/Tonne" title="Tonne">tonnes</a> annually, nearly all of which is exported to Russia and Eastern Europe. <a href="/wiki/Dian_Dian" class="mw-redirect" title="Dian Dian">Dian Dian</a>, a Guinean/<a href="/wiki/Ukraine" title="Ukraine">Ukrainian</a> joint bauxite venture, has a projected production rate of 1,000,000&#160;<a href="/wiki/Tonne" title="Tonne">t</a> (1,102,311 <a href="/wiki/Short_ton" title="Short ton">short tons</a>; 984,207 <a href="/wiki/Long_ton" title="Long ton">long tons</a>) per year, and is not expected to begin operation for several years. The <i><a href="/wiki/Alumina_Company_of_Guinea" title="Alumina Company of Guinea">Alumina Compagnie de Guinée</a></i> (ACG) which took over the former Friguia Consortium produced about 2.4&#160;million tonnes in 2004, as raw material for its alumina refinery. The refinery exports about 750,000 tonnes of alumina. Both Global Alumina and Alcoa-Alcan have signed conventions with the government of Guinea to build large alumina refineries, with a combined capacity of about 4 million tonnes per year.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Simandou_mine" title="Simandou mine">Simandou mine</a> is an <a href="/wiki/Iron_ore" title="Iron ore">iron ore</a> reserve.<sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> In March 2010, Anglo-Australian corporation <a href="/wiki/Rio_Tinto_Group" class="mw-redirect" title="Rio Tinto Group">Rio Tinto Group</a> and its biggest shareholder, <a href="/wiki/Aluminum_Corporation_of_China_Limited" title="Aluminum Corporation of China Limited">Aluminum Corporation of China Limited</a> (Chinalco), signed a preliminary agreement to develop Rio Tinto's iron ore project.<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> In 2017, the <a href="/wiki/Serious_Fraud_Office_(United_Kingdom)" title="Serious Fraud Office (United Kingdom)">Serious Fraud Office</a> (SFO), Britain's anti-fraud regulator, launched an official investigation into Rio Tinto's business and mining practices in Guinea.<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Tigui_Camara" title="Tigui Camara">Tigui Camara</a>, a former model, is the first woman in Guinea to own a mining company which is partially run as a social enterprise.<sup id="cite_ref-:3_96-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:3-96">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Oil">Oil</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Oil">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 2006, Guinea signed a <a href="/wiki/Production_sharing_agreement" title="Production sharing agreement">production sharing agreement</a> with Hyperdynamics Corporation of <a href="/wiki/Houston" title="Houston">Houston</a> to explore an offshore tract, and was then in partnership with <a href="/wiki/Dana_Petroleum" title="Dana Petroleum">Dana Petroleum</a> PLC (<a href="/wiki/Aberdeen" title="Aberdeen">Aberdeen</a>, United Kingdom). The initial well, the Sabu-1, was scheduled to begin drilling in October 2011, at a site in approximately 700&#160;metres of water. The Sabu-1 targeted a 4-way <a href="/wiki/Anticline" title="Anticline">anticline</a> prospect with <a href="/wiki/Upper_Cretaceous" class="mw-redirect" title="Upper Cretaceous">upper Cretaceous</a> sands, and was anticipated to be drilled to a total depth of 3,600&#160;meters.<sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Following the completion of exploratory drilling in 2012, the Sabu-1 well was not deemed commercially viable.<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> In November 2012, Hyperdynamics subsidiary SCS reached an agreement for a sale of 40% of the concession to <a href="/wiki/Tullow_Oil" title="Tullow Oil">Tullow Oil</a>, bringing ownership shares in the Guinea offshore tract to 37% Hyperdynamics, 40% Tullow Oil, and 23% Dana Petroleum.<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> Hyperdynamics will have until September 2016, under the current agreement, to begin drilling its next selected site, the Fatala <a href="/wiki/Cenomanian" title="Cenomanian">Cenomanian</a> <a href="/wiki/Turbidite" title="Turbidite">turbidite</a> fan prospect.<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Tourism">Tourism</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Tourism">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Le_%22Voile%22_de_la_mari%C3%A9e_%C3%A0_Kindia.png" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/Le_%22Voile%22_de_la_mari%C3%A9e_%C3%A0_Kindia.png/220px-Le_%22Voile%22_de_la_mari%C3%A9e_%C3%A0_Kindia.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="232" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/Le_%22Voile%22_de_la_mari%C3%A9e_%C3%A0_Kindia.png/330px-Le_%22Voile%22_de_la_mari%C3%A9e_%C3%A0_Kindia.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/Le_%22Voile%22_de_la_mari%C3%A9e_%C3%A0_Kindia.png/440px-Le_%22Voile%22_de_la_mari%C3%A9e_%C3%A0_Kindia.png 2x" data-file-width="979" data-file-height="1033" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Le_%22Voile%22_de_la_mari%C3%A9e_%C3%A0_Kindia.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The "Voile de la Mariée" (Bride's Veil) waterfall in Kindia</div></div></div>
<p>Among the attractions in Guinea are the waterfalls found mostly in the Basse Guinee (Lower Guinea) and Moyenne Guinee (Middle Guinea) regions. The Soumba cascade at the foot of Mount Kakoulima in Kindia, Voile de la Mariée (Bride's Veil) in Dubreka, the Kinkon cascades that are about 80&#160;m (260&#160;ft) high on the Kokoula River in the prefecture of Pita, the Kambadaga falls that can reach 100&#160;m (330&#160;ft) during the rainy season on the same river, the Ditinn &amp; Mitty waterfalls in Dalaba, and the Fetoré waterfalls and the stone bridge in the region of Labe are among water-related tourist sites.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Transport">Transport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Transport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Transport_in_Guinea" title="Transport in Guinea">Transport in Guinea</a></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Conakry_International_Airport" class="mw-redirect" title="Conakry International Airport">Conakry International Airport</a> is the largest airport in the country, with flights to other cities in Africa and to Europe.
</p><p>Built between 1904 and 1910, a railway once linked Conakry to <a href="/wiki/Kankan" title="Kankan">Kankan</a> via <a href="/wiki/Kouroussa" title="Kouroussa">Kouroussa</a> ceased operating in 1995<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> and had been dismantled altogether by 2007 with rails mostly stolen and/or sold for scrap. Plans had at 1 time been mooted for the passenger line to be rehabilitated as part of an iron-ore development master plan and while the start of work was announced in 2010, corruption charges led the whole master plan to be paused and the line was rebuilt as a 105&#160;km mineral railway, paralleling the older route as far as the mines of <a href="/wiki/Kalia,_Faranah" title="Kalia, Faranah">Kalia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> There is a state run mineral railway linking the bauxite mines of <a href="/wiki/Sangar%C3%A9di" title="Sangarédi">Sangarédi</a> to the port of <a href="/wiki/Kamsar" title="Kamsar">Kamsar</a> (137&#160;km) and a 1960s narrow-gauge line operated by Russian aluminium producer <a href="/wiki/RusAl" class="mw-redirect" title="RusAl">RusAl</a> to the mines at <a href="/wiki/Fria" title="Fria">Fria</a> (143&#160;km).
</p><p>As part of the plans to restart iron ore mining at <a href="/wiki/Simandou" title="Simandou">Simandou</a> blocks 1 and 2, the new development consortium pledged in 2019 to fund the construction of a new heavy-duty <a href="/wiki/Standard_gauge" class="mw-redirect" title="Standard gauge">standard gauge</a> railway to <a href="/wiki/Matakong" title="Matakong">Matakong</a> on the Atlantic coast where they would invest some US$20 billion in developing a deepwater port.<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> The 650&#160;km route is longer than an alternative heading south to the port of <a href="/wiki/Buchanan,_Liberia" title="Buchanan, Liberia">Buchanan, Liberia</a>, which was considered as an alternative in an October 2019 feasibility study.<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> However, the Matakong route would be entirely within Guinea and tied to an agricultural development corridor for citizens along the route.
</p><p>Some vehicles in Guinea are more than 20 years old, and cabs are any 4-door vehicle which the owner has designated as being for hire. Locals, nearly entirely without vehicles of their own, rely upon these taxis (which charge per seat) and small buses to take them around town and across the country. Horses and donkeys pull carts, primarily to transport construction materials.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demography">Demography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Demography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demography_of_Guinea" class="mw-redirect" title="Demography of Guinea">Demography of Guinea</a></div>
<p>The population of Guinea is estimated at 13.5&#160;million. <a href="/wiki/Conakry" title="Conakry">Conakry</a>, the capital and most populous city, is a hub of economy, commerce, education, and culture. In 2014, the <a href="/wiki/Total_fertility_rate" title="Total fertility rate">total fertility rate</a> (TFR) of Guinea was estimated at 4.93 children born per woman.<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div>

<p>The official language of Guinea is French. <a href="/wiki/Pular_language" title="Pular language">Pular</a> was spoken by 33.9% of the population in 2018 as their first or native language, followed by <a href="/wiki/Mandinka_language" title="Mandinka language">Mandingo</a>, with 29.4%. The third most spoken native language is the <a href="/wiki/Susu_language" title="Susu language">Susu</a>, spoken by 21.2% of the population in 2018 as their first language. Other languages spoken in Guinea as Guineans native language totalled 16% of the population in 2018, including <a href="/wiki/Kissi_language" title="Kissi language">Kissi</a> and <a href="/wiki/Kpelle_language" title="Kpelle language">Kpelle</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_Factbook1_1-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_Factbook1-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The population of Guinea comprises about 24 ethnic groups. The <a href="/wiki/Mandinka_people" title="Mandinka people">Mandinka</a>, also known as Mandingo or Malinké, comprise 29.4%<sup id="cite_ref-cia.gov_110-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia.gov-110">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> of the population and are mostly found in eastern Guinea concentrated around the <a href="/wiki/Kankan_Prefecture" title="Kankan Prefecture">Kankan</a> and <a href="/wiki/Kissidougou_Prefecture" title="Kissidougou Prefecture">Kissidougou</a> prefectures.<sup id="cite_ref-guinea_stats_13-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-guinea_stats-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Fula_people" title="Fula people">Fulas or Fulani</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-Fula_78-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Fula-78">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> comprise 33.4%<sup id="cite_ref-cia.gov_110-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia.gov-110">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> of the population and are mostly found in the <a href="/wiki/Futa_Djallon" class="mw-redirect" title="Futa Djallon">Futa Djallon</a> region. The <a href="/wiki/Soussou" class="mw-redirect" title="Soussou">Soussou</a>, comprising 21.2% of the population, are predominantly in western areas around the capital <a href="/wiki/Conakry" title="Conakry">Conakry</a>, <a href="/wiki/For%C3%A9cariah" title="Forécariah">Forécariah</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Kindia" title="Kindia">Kindia</a>. Smaller ethnic groups make up the remaining 16%<sup id="cite_ref-cia.gov_110-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia.gov-110">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> of the population, including <a href="/wiki/Kpelle_people" title="Kpelle people">Kpelle</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kissi_people" title="Kissi people">Kissi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Zialo" class="mw-redirect" title="Zialo">Zialo</a>, Toma and others.<sup id="cite_ref-guinea_stats_13-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-guinea_stats-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> Approximately 10,000 non-Africans live in Guinea, predominantly Lebanese, French, and other Europeans.<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Guinea" title="Religion in Guinea">Religion in Guinea</a></div>
<div class="barbox tright" style="overflow-x: auto;">
<div style="border:1px solid silver; font-size:88%; padding:0.4em; width:auto; background: white;">
<table style="text-align:left; border-collapse:collapse; width:100%;">
<tbody><tr style="background:#ddd"><th style="text-align:center;" colspan="5">Guinea religious sects<sup id="cite_ref-state.gov_112-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-state.gov-112">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup></th></tr>
<tr style="font-size:88%; height:4px;">
<td colspan="2" style="padding:0 4px; text-align:left;"><b>Religion</b></td>
<td style="width:100px; text-align:left;"></td>
<td colspan="2" style="padding:0 4px; width:1em; text-align:right;"><b>Percent</b></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="2" style="padding-left: 0.4em; padding-right: 0.4em; min-width: 8em;">Islam</td>
<td style="width: 100px; border-left: solid 1px silver; border-right: solid 1px silver;"><div style="background:lightgreen; width:85%; overflow: hidden;">&#8201;</div></td>
<td colspan="2" style="padding-left: 1.2em; padding-right: 0.4em; text-align: right;">85%</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="2" style="padding-left: 0.4em; padding-right: 0.4em; min-width: 8em;">Christianity</td>
<td style="width: 100px; border-left: solid 1px silver; border-right: solid 1px silver;"><div style="background:blue; width:8%; overflow: hidden;">&#8201;</div></td>
<td colspan="2" style="padding-left: 1.2em; padding-right: 0.4em; text-align: right;">8%</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td colspan="2" style="padding-left: 0.4em; padding-right: 0.4em; min-width: 8em;"><a href="/wiki/Traditional_African_religion" class="mw-redirect" title="Traditional African religion">Traditional African religion</a></td>
<td style="width: 100px; border-left: solid 1px silver; border-right: solid 1px silver;"><div style="background:brown; width:7%; overflow: hidden;">&#8201;</div></td>
<td colspan="2" style="padding-left: 1.2em; padding-right: 0.4em; text-align: right;">7%</td>
</tr>

</tbody></table>
</div>
</div>
<p>The population of Guinea is approximately 85% Muslim and 8% Christian, with 7% adhering to indigenous religious beliefs.<sup id="cite_ref-state.gov_112-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-state.gov-112">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> Some, both Muslim and Christian, have incorporated indigenous African beliefs into their outlook.<sup id="cite_ref-state.gov_112-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-state.gov-112">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The majority of Guinean Muslims are adherent to <a href="/wiki/Sunni_Islam" title="Sunni Islam">Sunni Islam</a>, of the <a href="/wiki/Maliki" class="mw-redirect" title="Maliki">Maliki</a> school of jurisprudence, influenced by <a href="/wiki/Sufism" title="Sufism">Sufism</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> Christian groups include <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Roman Catholics</a>, <a href="/wiki/Anglicanism" title="Anglicanism">Anglicans</a>, <a href="/wiki/Baptists" title="Baptists">Baptists</a>, <a href="/wiki/Seventh-day_Adventist_Church" title="Seventh-day Adventist Church">Seventh-day Adventists</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Evangelicalism" title="Evangelicalism">Evangelical</a> groups. <a href="/wiki/Jehovah%27s_Witnesses" title="Jehovah&#39;s Witnesses">Jehovah's Witnesses</a> are active in the country and recognized by the Government. There is a <a href="/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_Faith" title="Baháʼí Faith">Baháʼí Faith</a> community. There are numbers of <a href="/wiki/Hinduism" title="Hinduism">Hindus</a>, <a href="/wiki/Buddhism" title="Buddhism">Buddhists</a>, and traditional Chinese religious groups among the <a href="/wiki/Expatriate" title="Expatriate">expatriate</a> community.<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There were 3 days of ethno-religious fighting in the city of <a href="/wiki/Nzerekore" class="mw-redirect" title="Nzerekore">Nzerekore</a> in July 2013.<sup id="cite_ref-Guinea_2013_77-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Guinea_2013-77">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-bbc.com_115-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc.com-115">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> Fighting between ethnic <a href="/wiki/Kpelle_people" title="Kpelle people">Kpelle</a> who are Christian or animist, and ethnic Konianke who are Muslims and close to the larger <a href="/wiki/Malinke" class="mw-redirect" title="Malinke">Malinke</a> ethnic group, left at least 54 dead.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc.com_115-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc.com-115">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> The dead included people who were killed with machetes and burned alive.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc.com_115-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc.com-115">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> The violence ended after the Guinea military imposed a curfew, and President Conde made a televised appeal for calm.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc.com_115-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc.com-115">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Education_in_Guinea" title="Education in Guinea">Education in Guinea</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Guinea_schoolgirls.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/71/Guinea_schoolgirls.jpg/220px-Guinea_schoolgirls.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="215" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/71/Guinea_schoolgirls.jpg/330px-Guinea_schoolgirls.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/71/Guinea_schoolgirls.jpg/440px-Guinea_schoolgirls.jpg 2x" data-file-width="500" data-file-height="489" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Guinea_schoolgirls.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Schoolgirls in <a href="/wiki/Conakry" title="Conakry">Conakry</a></div></div></div>
<p>In 2010 it was estimated that 41% of adults were literate (52% of males and 30% of females).<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup>
Primary education is compulsory for 6 years.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> In 1999, primary school attendance was 40%. Children, particularly girls, are kept out of school to assist their parents with domestic work or agriculture,<sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> or to be married: Guinea has "one of the highest rates" of <a href="/wiki/Child_marriage" title="Child marriage">child marriage</a> in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Health">Health</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Health">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Health_in_Guinea" title="Health in Guinea">Health in Guinea</a></div>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Ebola">Ebola</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Ebola">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Ebola_virus_epidemic_in_West_Africa" class="mw-redirect" title="Ebola virus epidemic in West Africa">Ebola virus epidemic in West Africa</a></div>
<p>In 2014, an outbreak of the <a href="/wiki/Ebola_virus_epidemic_in_Guinea" title="Ebola virus epidemic in Guinea">Ebola virus occurred in Guinea</a>. In response, the health ministry banned <a href="/wiki/Bushmeat" title="Bushmeat">the sale and consumption of bats</a>, thought to be carriers of the disease. The virus eventually spread from rural areas to Conakry,<sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> and by June 2014 had spread to neighbouring countries - Sierra Leone and Liberia. In August 2014 Guinea closed its borders to Sierra Leone and Liberia to help contain the spread of the virus, as more new cases of the disease were being reported in those countries than in Guinea.
</p><p>The outbreak began in December in a village called Meliandou, southeastern Guinea, near the borders with Liberia and Sierra Leone. The first known case involved a 2-year-old child who died, after fever and vomiting and passing black stool, on 6 December. The child's mother died a week later, then a sister and a grandmother, all with symptoms that included fever, vomiting, and diarrhoea. Then, by way of care-giving visits or attendance at funerals, the outbreak spread to other villages.
</p><p>"Unsafe burials" is a source of the transmission of the disease. The World Health Organization (WHO) reported that the inability to engage with local communities hindered the ability of health workers to trace the origins and strains of the virus.<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>While WHO terminated the Public Health Emergency of International Concern (PHEIC) on 29 March 2016,<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> the Ebola Situation Report released on 30 March confirmed 5 more cases in the preceding 2 weeks, with viral sequencing relating 1 of the cases to the November 2014 outbreak.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Ebola epidemic affected the treatment of other diseases in Guinea. Healthcare visits by the population declined due to fear of infection and to mistrust in the health-care system, and the system's ability to provide routine health-care and HIV/AIDS treatments decreased due to the Ebola outbreak.<sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Ebola re-emerged in Guinea in January–February 2021.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Maternal_and_child_healthcare">Maternal and child healthcare</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Maternal and child healthcare">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The 2021 maternal mortality rate per 100,000 births for Guinea is 576.<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> This is compared with 680 in 2010, 859.9 in 2008 and 964.7 in 1990. The under 5 mortality rate, per 1,000 births is 146 and the neonatal mortality as a percentage of under 5's mortality is 29. In Guinea the number of midwives per 1,000 live births is 1 and the lifetime risk of death for pregnant women is 1 in 26.<sup id="cite_ref-SOWMY_127-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SOWMY-127">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> Guinea has the second highest prevalence of <a href="/wiki/Female_genital_mutilation" title="Female genital mutilation">female genital mutilation</a> in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span id="HIV.2FAIDS"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="HIV/AIDS">HIV/AIDS</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: HIV/AIDS">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/HIV/AIDS_in_Guinea" title="HIV/AIDS in Guinea">HIV/AIDS in Guinea</a></div>
<p>An estimated 170,000 adults and children were infected at the end of 2004.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> Surveillance surveys conducted in 2001 and 2002 show higher rates of HIV in urban areas than in rural areas. Prevalence was highest in <a href="/wiki/Conakry" title="Conakry">Conakry</a> (5%) and in the cities of the <a href="/wiki/Forest_Guinea" class="mw-redirect" title="Forest Guinea">Forest Guinea</a> region (7%) bordering <a href="/wiki/C%C3%B4te_d%27Ivoire" class="mw-redirect" title="Côte d&#39;Ivoire">Côte d'Ivoire</a>, <a href="/wiki/Liberia" title="Liberia">Liberia</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Sierra_Leone" title="Sierra Leone">Sierra Leone</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-usaid_132-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-usaid-132">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>HIV is spread primarily through multiple-partner heterosexual intercourse. Men and women are at nearly equal risk for HIV, with people aged 15 to 24 most vulnerable. Surveillance figures from 2001 to 2002 show the rates among commercial sex workers (42%), active military personnel (6.6%), truck drivers and bush taxi drivers (7.3%), miners (4.7%), and adults with tuberculosis (8.6%).<sup id="cite_ref-usaid_132-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-usaid-132">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Several factors were attributed to what fuel the HIV/AIDS epidemic in Guinea. They include unprotected sex, multiple sexual partners, illiteracy, endemic poverty, unstable borders, refugee migration, lack of civic responsibility, and scarce medical care and public services.<sup id="cite_ref-usaid_132-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-usaid-132">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Malnutrition">Malnutrition</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Malnutrition">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>A 2012 study reported malnutrition rates with levels ranging from 34% to 40% by region, and acute malnutrition rates above 10% in Upper Guinea's mining zones. The survey showed that 139,200 children underwent acute malnutrition, 609,696 underwent chronic malnutrition and further 1,592,892 have anemia. Degradation of care practices, limited access to medical services, inadequate hygiene practices and a lack of food diversity were said to explain these levels.<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Malaria">Malaria</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Malaria">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p><a href="/wiki/Malaria" title="Malaria">Malaria</a> is transmitted year-round, with peak transmission from July through October.<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> It is a cause of disability in Guinea.<sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="COVID-19_pandemic">COVID-19 pandemic</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: COVID-19 pandemic">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Guinea" title="COVID-19 pandemic in Guinea">COVID-19 pandemic in Guinea</a></div>
<p>The first case of <a href="/wiki/COVID-19" title="COVID-19">COVID-19</a> was reported in Guinea on 13 March <a href="/wiki/2020_in_Guinea" title="2020 in Guinea">2020</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-First_136-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-First-136">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> By the end of 2020 the total number of confirmed cases was 13,722. Of these, 13,141 had recovered, 500 were active, and 81 people had died.<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:302px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Guinea_Dinguiraye_market.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/77/Guinea_Dinguiraye_market.jpg/300px-Guinea_Dinguiraye_market.jpg" decoding="async" width="300" height="195" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/7/77/Guinea_Dinguiraye_market.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="450" data-file-height="293" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Guinea_Dinguiraye_market.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A market stall selling vegetables in <a href="/wiki/Dinguiraye_Prefecture" title="Dinguiraye Prefecture">Dinguiraye Prefecture</a>, Guinea</div></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sports">Sports</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Sports">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p><a href="/wiki/Association_football" title="Association football">Football</a> is the "most popular sport" in the country of Guinea,<sup id="cite_ref-Encyclopedia_138-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Encyclopedia-138">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> alongside <a href="/wiki/Basketball" title="Basketball">basketball</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Football operations are run by the <a href="/wiki/Guinean_Football_Federation" title="Guinean Football Federation">Guinean Football Federation</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup> The association administers the <a href="/wiki/Guinea_national_football_team" title="Guinea national football team">national football team</a>, and the national league.<sup id="cite_ref-Encyclopedia_138-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Encyclopedia-138">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> It was founded in 1960 and affiliated with <a href="/wiki/FIFA" title="FIFA">FIFA</a> since 1962<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> and with the <a href="/wiki/Confederation_of_African_Football" title="Confederation of African Football">Confederation of African Football</a> since 1963.<sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Guinea_national_football_team" title="Guinea national football team">Guinea national football team</a>, nicknamed <i>Syli nationale</i> (National Elephants), have played international football since 1962.<sup id="cite_ref-Encyclopedia_138-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Encyclopedia-138">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> Their first opponent was East Germany.<sup id="cite_ref-Encyclopedia_138-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Encyclopedia-138">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> They have yet to reach <a href="/wiki/FIFA_World_Cup" title="FIFA World Cup">World Cup</a> finals, and were runners-up to Morocco in the <a href="/wiki/Africa_Cup_of_Nations" title="Africa Cup of Nations">Africa Cup of Nations</a> in 1976.<sup id="cite_ref-Encyclopedia_138-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Encyclopedia-138">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Guin%C3%A9e_Championnat_National" title="Guinée Championnat National">Guinée Championnat National</a> is the top division of Guinean football. Since it was established in 1965, 3 teams have dominated in winning the <a href="/wiki/Guin%C3%A9e_Coupe_Nationale" title="Guinée Coupe Nationale">Guinée Coupe Nationale</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-RSSSF_143-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RSSSF-143">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Horoya_AC" title="Horoya AC">Horoya AC</a> has at least 16 titles and is the 2017–2018 champion. <a href="/wiki/Hafia_FC" title="Hafia FC">Hafia FC</a> (known as Conakry II in 1960s) has at least 15 titles, having dominated in 1960s and 70s. <a href="/wiki/AS_Kaloum_Star" title="AS Kaloum Star">AS Kaloum Star</a> (known as Conakry I in the 1960s) has at least 13 titles. All 3 teams are based in the capital, <a href="/wiki/Conakry" title="Conakry">Conakry</a>.
</p><p>Hafia FC won the <a href="/wiki/African_Cup_of_Champions_Clubs" class="mw-redirect" title="African Cup of Champions Clubs">African Cup of Champions Clubs</a> 3 times, in <a href="/wiki/1972_African_Cup_of_Champions_Clubs" title="1972 African Cup of Champions Clubs">1972</a>, <a href="/wiki/1975_African_Cup_of_Champions_Clubs" title="1975 African Cup of Champions Clubs">1975</a> and <a href="/wiki/1977_African_Cup_of_Champions_Clubs" title="1977 African Cup of Champions Clubs">1977</a>, while Horoya AC won the <a href="/wiki/1978_African_Cup_Winners%27_Cup" title="1978 African Cup Winners&#39; Cup">1978 African Cup Winners' Cup</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Polygamy">Polygamy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Polygamy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Polygamy_in_Guinea" title="Polygamy in Guinea">Polygamy in Guinea</a></div>
<p>Polygamy is generally prohibited by law in Guinea, and there are exceptions.<sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup> In 2020, it was estimated that about 26% of marriages were polygamous (29% Muslim and 10% Christian).<sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cuisine">Cuisine</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Cuisine">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Cuisine_of_Guinea" title="Cuisine of Guinea">Cuisine of Guinea</a></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Guinean_cuisine" class="mw-redirect" title="Guinean cuisine">Guinean cuisine</a> varies by region with rice as a staple. <a href="/wiki/Cassava" title="Cassava">Cassava</a> is consumed.<sup id="cite_ref-friends_147-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-friends-147">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> Part of <a href="/wiki/West_African_cuisine" title="West African cuisine">West African cuisine</a>, the foods of Guinea include <a href="/w/index.php?title=Y%C3%A9tiss%C3%A9&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Yétissé (page does not exist)">yétissé</a>, <a href="/wiki/Peanut_sauce" title="Peanut sauce">peanut sauce </a>, <a href="/w/index.php?title=Okra_sauce&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Okra sauce (page does not exist)">okra sauce</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tapalapa_bread" title="Tapalapa bread">tapalapa bread</a>. In rural areas, food is eaten from a "large serving dish" and eaten by hand outside of homes.<sup id="cite_ref-dc_148-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-dc-148">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="See_also">See also</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Guinea&amp;action=edit&amp;section=34" title="Edit section: See also">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
